          <p>A corporation shall indemnify those persons identified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.521">317A.521</a> against certain expenses and liabilities only as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.521">317A.521</a> and may indemnify other persons.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.161.22">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 22.<span class="headnote">Assumed names.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may conduct all or part of its business under one or more assumed names as provided in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/333.001">333.001</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/333.06">333.06</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.161.23">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 23.<span class="headnote">May take and hold trust property.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may take, receive, and hold real and personal property, including the principal and interest of money or other fund, that is given, conveyed, bequeathed, devised to, or vested in the corporation in trust where the corporation or a related organization has a vested or contingent interest in the trust.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.161.24">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 24.<span class="headnote">May invest trust property.</span></h2>
          <p>Except where the trust instrument prescribes otherwise, a corporation may invest trust property or its proceeds in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/501C.0901">501C.0901</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.161.25">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 25.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may be a member of another foreign or domestic nonprofit corporation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.161.26">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 26.<span class="headnote">Dissolution.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may dissolve and wind up.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.161.27">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 27.<span class="headnote">Merger and consolidation.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may merge and consolidate with other domestic or foreign nonprofit corporations organized for related purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.161.28">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 28.<span class="headnote">Other powers.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation has other powers necessary or convenient to effect a lawful purpose for which the corporation is incorporated.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.161.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.22.0">1989 c 304 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/340/#laws.2.8.0">1989 c 340 art 2 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/314/#laws.0.3.0">1996 c 314 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/5/#laws.15.3.0">2015 c 5 art 15 s 3</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.163">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.163 CORPORATE SEAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.163.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Seal not required.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may, but need not, have a corporate seal. The use or nonuse of a corporate seal does not affect the validity, recordability, or enforceability of a document or act. If a corporation has a corporate seal, the use of the seal by the corporation on a document is not necessary.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.163.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Required words; use.</span></h2>
          <p>If a corporation has a corporate seal, the seal may consist of a mechanical imprinting device, or a rubber stamp with a facsimile of the seal affixed on it, or a facsimile or reproduction of either. The seal may include a part or all of the name of the corporation and a combination, derivation, or abbreviation of either or both of the phrases "a Minnesota Nonprofit Corporation" and "Corporate Seal." If a corporation has a corporate seal, it or a facsimile of it may be affixed, engraved, printed, placed, stamped with indelible ink, or in any other manner reproduced on any document.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.163.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.23.0">1989 c 304 s 23</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.165">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.165 EFFECT OF LACK OF POWER; ULTRA VIRES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.165.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in this section, the doing, continuing, or performing by a corporation of an act, or an executed or wholly or partially executory contract, conveyance, or transfer to or by the corporation, if otherwise lawful, is not invalid because the corporation was without the power under this chapter or its articles or bylaws to do, continue, or perform the act, contract, conveyance, or transfer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.165.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Action by member.</span></h2>
          <p>At least 50 members with voting rights or ten percent of the members with voting rights, whichever is less, may bring an action against the corporation to enjoin the doing, continuing, or performing of an unauthorized act, contract, conveyance, or transfer. If the unauthorized act, continuation, or performance sought to be enjoined is being, or to be, performed or made pursuant to a contract to which the corporation is a party, the court may, if just and reasonable in the circumstances, set aside and enjoin the performance of the contract and allow to the corporation or to the other parties to the contract compensation for the loss or damage sustained as a result of the action of the court in setting aside and enjoining the performance of the contract.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.165.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Action by corporation.</span></h2>
          <p>The corporation may bring an action, directly or through a director or member with voting rights in a representative or derivative suit, against the incumbent or former officers or directors of the corporation for exceeding or violating their authority.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.165.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.24.0">1989 c 304 s 24</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ORGANIZATION; BYLAWS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.171">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.171 ORGANIZATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.171.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Role of incorporators.</span></h2>
          <p>If the first board is not named in the articles, the incorporators may elect the first board or may act as directors with the powers, rights, duties, and liabilities of directors, until directors are elected. Upon the election and qualification of the first director, the power of the incorporator or incorporators terminates. If the persons who are to serve as directors until the first annual meeting have been named in the articles of incorporation, the power of the incorporator or incorporators to act for the corporation terminates upon filing of the articles.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.171.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Meeting.</span></h2>
          <p>After the issuance of the certificate of incorporation, the incorporators or the directors named in the articles shall, within a reasonable time, hold an organizational meeting at the call of a majority of the incorporators or of the directors named in the articles, or take written action, for the purposes of electing directors, transacting business, and taking actions necessary or appropriate to complete the organization of the corporation. If a meeting is held, the persons calling the meeting shall give at least three days' notice of the meeting to each incorporator or director named, stating the date, time, and place of the meeting.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.171.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.25.0">1989 c 304 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.3.0">2017 c 17 s 3</a>,4</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.181">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.181 BYLAWS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.181.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may, but need not, have bylaws. Bylaws may contain any provision relating to the management or regulation of the affairs of the corporation consistent with law or the articles, including but not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) the number of directors, and the qualifications, manner of election, powers, duties, and compensation, if any, of directors;</p>
          <p>(2) the qualifications of members;</p>
          <p>(3) different classes of membership;</p>
          <p>(4) the manner of admission, withdrawal, suspension, and expulsion of members;</p>
          <p>(5) property, voting, and other rights and privileges of members;</p>
          <p>(6) the appointment and authority of committees;</p>
          <p>(7) the appointment or election, duties, compensation, and tenure of officers;</p>
          <p>(8) the time, place, and manner of calling, conducting, and giving notice of member, board, and committee meetings, or of conducting mail ballots;</p>
          <p>(9) the making of reports and financial statements to members; or</p>
          <p>(10) the number establishing a quorum for meetings of members and the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.181.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Adoption and amendment; no members with voting rights.</span></h2>
          <p>Initial bylaws may be adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.171">317A.171</a> by a majority of the incorporators or the first board. The bylaws may be amended in the manner provided in the articles or bylaws. In the absence of such provision, a majority of directors may amend or repeal the bylaws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.181.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Adoption and amendment; members with voting rights.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Unless reserved by the articles to the members with voting rights, initial bylaws may be adopted under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.171">317A.171</a> by a majority of the incorporators or by the first board. Unless reserved by the articles to the members, the power to adopt, amend, or repeal the bylaws is vested in the board. The power of the board is subject to the power of the members with voting rights under paragraph (b) to adopt, amend, or repeal bylaws adopted, amended, or repealed by the board. The bylaws may be amended in the manner provided in the articles or bylaws. In the absence of such provision, the following bylaw amendments are subject to approval by the members with voting rights: fixing a quorum for meetings of members; prescribing procedures for removing directors or filling vacancies in the board; fixing the number of directors or their classifications, qualifications, or terms of office prescribing procedures for removing or adding members; and increasing or decreasing the vote required for a member action. The board may adopt or amend a bylaw provision to increase the number of directors without member approval.</p>
          <p>(b) Unless the articles or bylaws provide otherwise, at least 50 members with voting rights or ten percent of the members with voting rights, whichever is less, may propose a resolution for action by the members to adopt, amend, or repeal bylaws adopted, amended, or repealed by the board. The resolution must contain the provisions proposed for adoption, amendment, or repeal. The limitations and procedures for submitting, considering, and adopting the resolution are the same as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.133">317A.133</a>, for amendment of the articles, except that board approval is not required.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.181.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.26.0">1989 c 304 s 26</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.13.0">1990 c 488 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.18.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 18</a>,19</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">BOARD</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.201">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.201 BOARD.</h1>
        <p>The business and affairs of a corporation must be managed by or under the direction of a board of directors. All directors are entitled to vote and have equal rights and preferences except as otherwise provided in the articles or bylaws. The members of the first board may be named in the articles, designated or appointed pursuant to the articles, or elected by the incorporators under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.171">317A.171</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.201.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.27.0">1989 c 304 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.14.0">1990 c 488 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.203">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.203 NUMBER.</h1>
        <p>A board of directors must consist of three or more individuals, with the number specified in or fixed in accordance with the articles or bylaws. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.227">317A.227</a>, if the power to elect or appoint directors is vested in the board of directors and if the number of directors is fewer than three, or such greater minimum number set forth in the articles or bylaws, a majority of the directors in office may appoint or elect the number of additional directors necessary to increase the board to three directors or such greater minimum set forth in the articles or bylaws.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.203.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.28.0">1989 c 304 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/0/208/#laws.0.21.0">2001 c 208 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.20.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.5.0">2017 c 17 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.205">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.205 QUALIFICATIONS; ELECTION.</h1>
        <p>The qualifications and method of election or appointment of directors may be imposed by or in the manner provided in the articles or bylaws, provided that directors must be natural persons and a majority of the directors must be adults. The articles or bylaws may provide for ex officio directors who are directors because they hold another office or position.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.205.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.29.0">1989 c 304 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.15.0">1990 c 488 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.207">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.207 TERMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.207.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Length.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Directors are elected or appointed and hold office for fixed terms provided for in the articles or bylaws. A term of a director, other than an ex officio director, may not exceed ten years. If the articles or bylaws do not provide for a fixed term, the term is one year. An ex officio director serves as long as the director holds the office or position designated in the articles or bylaws.</p>
          <p>(b) Unless the articles or bylaws provide otherwise, a director holds office until expiration of the term for which the director was elected or appointed and until a successor is elected and qualified, or until the earlier death, resignation, removal, or disqualification of the director.</p>
          <p>(c) A decrease in the number of directors or term of office does not shorten an incumbent director's term.</p>
          <p>(d) Except as provided in the articles or bylaws, the term of a director filling a vacancy expires at the end of the unexpired term that the director is filling.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.207.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Staggered terms.</span></h2>
          <p>The articles or bylaws may provide for staggering the terms of directors by dividing the total number of directors into groups. The terms of office of the groups need not be uniform.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.207.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.30.0">1989 c 304 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.16.0">1990 c 488 s 16</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.209">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.209 EXPIRATION OF TERM; ACTS NOT VOID OR VOIDABLE.</h1>
        <p>The expiration of a director's term with or without the election of a qualified successor does not make prior or later acts of the officers or the board void or voidable.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.209.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.31.0">1989 c 304 s 31</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.211">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.211 COMPENSATION.</h1>
        <p>Subject to a limitation in the articles or bylaws, the board may fix the compensation of directors.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.211.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.32.0">1989 c 304 s 32</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.213">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.213 CLASSIFICATION OF DIRECTORS.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (b), directors may be divided into classes.</p>
        <p>(b) Directors of a corporation described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811#stat.317A.811.1">317A.811, subdivision 1</a>, may not vote by class except when the articles or bylaws provide that only one class of directors may vote on a particular matter.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.213.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.33.0">1989 c 304 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.17.0">1990 c 488 s 17</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.215">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.215 CUMULATIVE VOTING FOR DIRECTORS.</h1>
        <p>Unless the articles provide otherwise, there is no cumulative voting.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.215.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.34.0">1989 c 304 s 34</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.221">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.221 RESIGNATION.</h1>
        <p>(a) A director may resign at any time by giving written notice to the corporation. The resignation is effective without acceptance when the notice is given to the corporation, unless a later effective time is specified in the notice.</p>
        <p>(b) If a resignation is made effective at a later date, the board may fill the pending vacancy before the effective date if the board provides that the successor does not take office until the effective date.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.221.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.35.0">1989 c 304 s 35</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.223">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.223 REMOVAL OF DIRECTORS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.223.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Modification.</span></h2>
          <p>The provisions of this section apply unless a different method of removal is provided for in the articles or bylaws.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.223.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Removal by directors when there are members with voting rights.</span></h2>
          <p>If there are members with voting rights, a director may be removed at any time, with or without cause, if:</p>
          <p>(1) the director was named by the board to fill a vacancy;</p>
          <p>(2) the members with voting rights have not elected directors in the interval between the time of the appointment to fill the vacancy and the time of the removal; and</p>
          <p>(3) a majority of the remaining directors present affirmatively vote to remove the director.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.223.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Removal by directors when there are no members with voting rights.</span></h2>
          <p>If there are no members with voting rights, a director may be removed at any time, with or without cause, by those directors eligible to elect the director.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.223.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Removal by members with voting rights.</span></h2>
          <p>A director may be removed at any time, with or without cause, by those members eligible to elect the director.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.223.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.36.0">1989 c 304 s 36</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.225">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.225 REMOVAL OF APPOINTED DIRECTORS.</h1>
        <p>Except as otherwise provided in the articles or bylaws, an appointed director may be removed without cause by the person appointing the director. The person removing the director shall do so by giving written notice of the removal to the director and either the presiding officer of the board or the corporation's president or secretary. A removal is effective when the notice is effective unless the notice states a future effective date.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.225.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.37.0">1989 c 304 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.18.0">1990 c 488 s 18</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.227">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.227 VACANCIES.</h1>
        <p>(a) Unless the articles or bylaws provide otherwise, and except as provided in paragraphs (b), (c), and (d), if a vacancy occurs on the board of directors, including a vacancy resulting from an increase in the number of directors:</p>
        <p>(1) the members with voting rights, if any, may fill the vacancy; or</p>
        <p>(2) the remaining members of the board, though less than a quorum, may fill the vacancy.</p>
        <p>(b) If a vacant office was held by a director elected by a class, chapter, or other organizational unit or by region or other geographic grouping, only members with voting rights of the class, chapter, unit, or grouping are entitled to vote to fill the vacancy.</p>
        <p>(c) If a vacant office was held by an appointed director, only the person who appointed the director may fill the vacancy.</p>
        <p>(d) A vacancy that will occur at a specific later date may be filled before the vacancy occurs but the new director may not take office until the vacancy occurs.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.227.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.38.0">1989 c 304 s 38</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/503/#laws.0.4.0">1992 c 503 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.21.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 21</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.231">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.231 BOARD MEETINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.231.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Time; place.</span></h2>
          <p>Meetings of the board may be held as provided in the articles or bylaws in or out of this state. Unless the articles or bylaws provide otherwise, a meeting of the board must be held at least once per year. If the articles or bylaws or the board fail to select a place or method for selecting a place for a meeting, the meeting must be held at the registered office.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.231.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 2000 [Paragraph (b) renumbered subd 3]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.231.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Meetings solely by means of remote communication.</span></h2>
          <p>Any meeting among directors may be conducted solely by one or more means of remote communication through which all of the directors may participate in the meeting, if the same notice is given of the meeting required by subdivision 4, and if the number of directors participating in the meeting is sufficient to constitute a quorum at a meeting. Participation in a meeting by that means constitutes presence at the meeting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.231.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 2000 [Renumbered subd 4]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.231.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Participation in meetings by means of remote communication.</span></h2>
          <p>A director may participate in a board meeting by means of conference telephone or, if authorized by the board, by such other means of remote communication, in each case through which that director, other directors so participating, and all directors physically present at the meeting may participate with each other during the meeting. Participation in a meeting by that means constitutes presence at the meeting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.231.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>MS 2000 [Renumbered subd 5]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.231.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Calling meetings; notice.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Unless the articles or bylaws provide otherwise, a director may call a board meeting by giving five days' notice to all directors of the date, time, and place of the meeting. The notice must contain the substance of the proposed amendment to the articles but otherwise need not state the purpose of the meeting unless the articles or bylaws require it.</p>
          <p>(b) If the day or date, time, and place of a board meeting have been provided in the articles or bylaws, or announced at a previous meeting of the board, notice is not required. Notice of an adjourned meeting need not be given other than by announcement at the meeting at which adjournment is taken.</p>
          <p>(c) Any notice to a director given under any provision of this chapter, the articles, or the bylaws by a form of electronic communication consented to by the director to whom the notice is given is effective when given. The notice is deemed given if by:</p>
          <p>(1) facsimile communication, when directed to a telephone number at which the director has consented to receive notice;</p>
          <p>(2) electronic mail, when directed to an electronic mail address at which the director has consented to receive notice;</p>
          <p>(3) a posting on an electronic network on which the director has consented to receive notice, together with a separate notice to the director of the specific posting, upon the later of:</p>
          <p>(i) the posting; or</p>
          <p>(ii) the giving of the separate notice; and</p>
          <p>(4) any other form of electronic communication by which the director has consented to receive notice, when directed to the director.</p>
          <p class="bl">An affidavit of the secretary, other authorized officer, or authorized agent of the corporation, that the notice has been given by a form of electronic communication is, in the absence of fraud, prima facie evidence of the facts stated in the affidavit.</p>
          <p>(d) Consent by a director to notice given by electronic communication may be given in writing or by authenticated electronic communication. Any consent so given may be relied upon until revoked by the director, provided that no revocation affects the validity of any notice given before receipt of revocation of the consent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.231.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Waiver of notice.</span></h2>
          <p>A director may waive notice of a meeting of the board. A waiver of notice by a director entitled to notice is effective whether given before, at, or after the meeting, and whether given in writing, orally, by authenticated electronic communication, or by attendance. Attendance by a director at a meeting is a waiver of notice of that meeting, unless the director objects at the beginning of the meeting to the transaction of business because the meeting is not lawfully called or convened and does not participate in the meeting.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.231.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.39.0">1989 c 304 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/311/#laws.3.5.0">2002 c 311 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/199/#laws.14.32.0">2004 c 199 art 14 s 32</a>,33; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.22.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 22</a>,23</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.235">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.235 QUORUM.</h1>
        <p>A majority, or a larger or smaller proportion or number provided in the articles or bylaws but not less than one-third, of the directors currently holding office is a quorum for the transaction of business. In the absence of a quorum, a majority of the directors present may adjourn a meeting from time to time until a quorum is present. If a quorum is present when a duly called or held meeting is convened, the directors present may continue to transact business until adjournment, even though the withdrawal of directors originally present leaves less than the proportion or number otherwise required for a quorum.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.235.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.40.0">1989 c 304 s 40</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.237">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.237 ACT OF THE BOARD.</h1>
        <p>The board shall take action by the affirmative vote of a majority of directors with voting rights present and entitled to vote at a duly held meeting, unless this chapter or the articles or bylaws require the affirmative vote of a larger proportion or number. Proxy voting is not permitted.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.237.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.41.0">1989 c 304 s 41</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.19.0">1990 c 488 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.24.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 24</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.239">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.239 ACTION WITHOUT MEETING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.239.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Method.</span></h2>
          <p>An action required or permitted to be taken at a board meeting may be taken by written action signed, or consented to by authenticated electronic communication, by all of the directors. If the articles so provide, an action, other than an action requiring approval of members with voting rights, may be taken by written action signed, or consented to by authenticated electronic communication, by the number of directors that would be required to take the same action at a meeting of the board at which all directors were present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.239.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Effective time.</span></h2>
          <p>The written action is effective when signed, or consented to by authenticated electronic communication, by the required number of directors, unless a different effective time is provided in the written action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.239.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notice; liability.</span></h2>
          <p>When written action is permitted to be taken by less than all directors, all directors must be notified immediately of its text and effective date. Failure to provide the notice does not invalidate the written action. A director who does not sign or consent to the written action is not liable for the action or actions taken thereby.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.239.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.42.0">1989 c 304 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/311/#laws.3.6.0">2002 c 311 art 3 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.25.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 25</a>,26</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.241">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.241 COMMITTEES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.241.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p>A resolution approved by the affirmative vote of a majority of the board may establish committees having the authority of the board in the management of the business of the corporation only to the extent provided in the resolution. Committees may include a special litigation committee consisting of one or more independent directors or other independent persons to consider legal rights or remedies of the corporation and whether those rights and remedies should be pursued. Committees other than special litigation committees are subject at all times to the direction and control of the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.241.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Membership.</span></h2>
          <p>Committee members must be natural persons. Unless the articles or bylaws provide otherwise, a committee must consist of one or more persons, who need not be directors, appointed by the affirmative vote of a majority of the directors present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.241.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Procedure.</span></h2>
          <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.231">317A.231</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.239">317A.239</a> apply to committees and members of committees to the same extent as those sections apply to the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.241.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Minutes.</span></h2>
          <p>Minutes, if any, of committee meetings must be made available upon request to members of the committee and to a director.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.241.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Standard of conduct.</span></h2>
          <p>The establishment of, delegation of authority to, and action by a committee does not alone constitute compliance by a director with the standard of conduct set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.251">317A.251</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.241.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Committee members considered directors.</span></h2>
          <p>Committee members are considered to be directors for purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.251">317A.251</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.255">317A.255</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.521">317A.521</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.241.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Subcommittees.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise provided in the articles, the bylaws, or the resolution of the board establishing the committee, a committee may create one or more subcommittees and may delegate to a subcommittee any or all of the authority of the committee. In this chapter, unless the language or context clearly indicates that a different meaning is intended, any reference to a committee is deemed to include a subcommittee, and any reference to a committee member is deemed to include a subcommittee member.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.241.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.43.0">1989 c 304 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.27.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 27</a>,28; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.6.0">2017 c 17 s 6</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.251">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.251 STANDARD OF CONDUCT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.251.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Standard; liability.</span></h2>
          <p>A director shall discharge the duties of the position of director in good faith, in a manner the director reasonably believes to be in the best interests of the corporation, and with the care an ordinarily prudent person in a like position would exercise under similar circumstances. A person who so performs those duties is not liable by reason of being or having been a director of the corporation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.251.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reliance.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A director is entitled to rely on information, opinions, reports, or statements, including financial statements and other financial data, in each case prepared or presented by:</p>
          <p>(1) one or more officers or employees of the corporation whom the director reasonably believes to be reliable and competent in the matters presented;</p>
          <p>(2) counsel, public accountants, or other persons as to matters that the director reasonably believes are within the person's professional or expert competence; or</p>
          <p>(3) a committee of the board upon which the director does not serve, duly established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.241">317A.241</a>, as to matters within its designated authority, if the director reasonably believes the committee to merit confidence.</p>
          <p>(b) Paragraph (a) does not apply to a director who has actual knowledge concerning the matter in question that makes the reliance otherwise permitted by paragraph (a) unwarranted.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.251.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Presumption of assent; dissent.</span></h2>
          <p>A director who is present at a meeting of the board when an action is approved by the board is presumed to have assented to the action approved, unless the director:</p>
          <p>(1) objects at the beginning of the meeting to the transaction of business because the meeting is not lawfully called or convened and does not participate in the meeting, in which case the director is not considered to be present at the meeting for purposes of this chapter;</p>
          <p>(2) votes against the action at the meeting; or</p>
          <p>(3) is prohibited from voting on the action by the articles or bylaws or as a result of a decision to approve, ratify, or authorize a transaction pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.255">317A.255</a> or a conflict of interest policy adopted by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.251.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Not considered trustee.</span></h2>
          <p>A director, regardless of how identified, is not considered to be a trustee with respect to the corporation or with respect to property held or administered by the corporation, including without limit, property that may be subject to restrictions imposed by the donor or transferor of the property.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.251.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.44.0">1989 c 304 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.20.0">1990 c 488 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/503/#laws.0.5.0">1992 c 503 s 5</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.255">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.255 DIRECTOR CONFLICTS OF INTEREST.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.255.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Conflict; procedure when conflict arises.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A contract or other transaction between a corporation and: (1) its director or a member of the family of its director; (2) a director of a related organization, or a member of the family of a director of a related organization; or (3) an organization in or of which the corporation's director, or a member of the family of its director, is a director, officer, or legal representative or has a material financial interest; is not void or voidable because the director or the other individual or organization are parties or because the director is present at the meeting of the members or the board or a committee at which the contract or transaction is authorized, approved, or ratified, if a requirement of paragraph (b) is satisfied.</p>
          <p>(b) A contract or transaction described in paragraph (a) is not void or voidable if:</p>
          <p>(1) the contract or transaction was, and the person asserting the validity of the contract or transaction has the burden of establishing that the contract or transaction was, fair and reasonable as to the corporation when it was authorized, approved, or ratified;</p>
          <p>(2) the material facts as to the contract or transaction and as to the director's interest are fully disclosed or known to the members and the contract or transaction is approved in good faith by two-thirds of the members entitled to vote, not counting any vote that the interested director might otherwise have, or the unanimous affirmative vote of all members, whether or not entitled to vote;</p>
          <p>(3) the material facts as to the contract or transaction and as to the director's interest are fully disclosed or known to the board or a committee, and the board or committee authorizes, approves, or ratifies the contract or transaction in good faith by a majority of the directors or committee members currently holding office, provided that the interested director or directors may not vote and are not considered present for purposes of a quorum. If, as a result, the number of remaining directors is not sufficient to reach a quorum, a quorum for the purpose of considering the contract or transaction is the number of remaining directors or committee members; or</p>
          <p>(4) the contract or transaction is a merger or consolidation described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.601">317A.601</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.255.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Material financial interest.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section:</p>
          <p>(1) a director does not have a material financial interest in a resolution fixing the compensation of the director or fixing the compensation of another director as a director, officer, employee, or agent of the corporation, even though the first director is also receiving compensation from the corporation; and</p>
          <p>(2) a director has a material financial interest in an organization in which the director, or a member of the family of the director, has a material financial interest.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.255.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exception.</span></h2>
          <p>The procedures described under subdivision 1, clauses (1) to (3), are not required if the contract or other transaction is between related organizations.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.255.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Member of the family.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, a member of the family of a director is a spouse, parent, child, spouse of a child, brother, sister, or spouse of a brother or sister.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.255.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.45.0">1989 c 304 s 45</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/503/#laws.0.6.0">1992 c 503 s 6</a>-8; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.29.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/106/#laws.0.12.0">2011 c 106 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.257">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.257 UNPAID DIRECTORS; LIABILITY FOR DAMAGES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.257.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in subdivision 2, a person who serves without compensation as a director, officer, trustee, member, or agent of an organization exempt from state income taxation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/290.05#stat.290.05.2">290.05, subdivision 2</a>, or who serves without compensation as a fire chief of a nonprofit fire-fighting corporation or municipal volunteer fire department, or of a public corporation established by law but not considered a municipality, is not civilly liable for an act or omission by that person if the act or omission was in good faith, was within the scope of the person's responsibilities as a director, officer, trustee, member, agent, or fire chief of the organization, and did not constitute willful or reckless misconduct.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.257.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Exceptions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Subdivision 1 does not apply to:</p>
          <p>(1) an action or proceeding brought by the attorney general for a breach of a fiduciary duty as a director;</p>
          <p>(2) a cause of action to the extent it is based on federal law;</p>
          <p>(3) a cause of action based on the person's express contractual obligation; or</p>
          <p>(4) an action or proceeding based on a breach of public pension plan fiduciary responsibility.</p>
          <p>(b) Subdivision 1 does not limit an individual's liability for physical injury to the person of another or for wrongful death that is personally and directly caused by the individual, nor the liability of a municipality arising out of the performance of firefighting or related activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.257.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Definition.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this section, the term "compensation" means any thing of value received for services rendered, except:</p>
          <p>(1) reimbursement for expenses actually incurred;</p>
          <p>(2) a per diem in an amount not more than the per diem authorized for state advisory councils and committees under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.059#stat.15.059.3">15.059, subdivision 3</a>; or</p>
          <p>(3) payment by an organization of insurance premiums on behalf of a person who is or was a director, officer, trustee, member, or agent of an organization, or who, while a director, officer, trustee, member, or agent of the organization, is or was serving at the request of the organization as a director, officer, partner, trustee, employee, or agent of another organization or employee benefit plan against liability asserted against and incurred by the person in or arising from that capacity.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.257.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.46.0">1989 c 304 s 46</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">OFFICERS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.301">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.301 OFFICERS REQUIRED.</h1>
        <p>A corporation must have one or more natural persons exercising the functions of the offices of president and treasurer, however designated.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.301.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.47.0">1989 c 304 s 47</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.21.0">1990 c 488 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.30.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 30</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.305">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.305 DUTIES OF REQUIRED OFFICERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.305.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Presumption; modification.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless the articles, the bylaws, or a resolution adopted by the board and consistent with the articles or bylaws provide otherwise, the president and treasurer have the duties in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.305.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">President.</span></h2>
          <p>The president shall:</p>
          <p>(1) have general active management of the business of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(2) when present, preside at meetings of the board and of the members;</p>
          <p>(3) see that orders and resolutions of the board are carried into effect;</p>
          <p>(4) sign and deliver in the name of the corporation deeds, mortgages, bonds, contracts, or other instruments pertaining to the business of the corporation, except in cases in which the authority to sign and deliver is required by law to be exercised by another person or is expressly delegated by the articles or bylaws or by the board to another officer or agent of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(5) maintain records of and, when necessary, certify proceedings of the board and the members; and</p>
          <p>(6) perform other duties prescribed by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.305.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Treasurer.</span></h2>
          <p>The treasurer shall:</p>
          <p>(1) keep accurate financial records for the corporation;</p>
          <p>(2) deposit money, drafts, and checks in the name of and to the credit of the corporation in the banks and depositories designated by the board;</p>
          <p>(3) endorse for deposit notes, checks, and drafts received by the corporation as ordered by the board, making proper vouchers for the deposit;</p>
          <p>(4) disburse corporate funds and issue checks and drafts in the name of the corporation, as ordered by the board;</p>
          <p>(5) upon request, provide the president and the board an account of transactions by the treasurer and of the financial condition of the corporation; and</p>
          <p>(6) perform other duties prescribed by the board or by the president.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.305.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.48.0">1989 c 304 s 48</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.311">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.311 OTHER OFFICERS.</h1>
        <p>Except to the extent that the articles or bylaws provide that the members may exercise the powers under this section, the board may elect or appoint, in a manner set forth in the articles or bylaws or in a resolution adopted by the board, other officers the board considers necessary for the operation and management of the corporation, each of whom has the powers, rights, duties, responsibilities, and terms in office provided for in the articles or bylaws or determined by the board. Unless reserved to the members with voting rights, to the extent authorized in the articles, the bylaws, or a resolution approved by the affirmative vote of a majority of the directors present, the president may appoint one or more officers, other than the treasurer.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.311.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.49.0">1989 c 304 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.22.0">1990 c 488 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.31.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.7.0">2017 c 17 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.315">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.315 MULTIPLE OFFICES.</h1>
        <p>Unless the articles or bylaws provide otherwise, any number of offices or functions of those offices may be held or exercised by the same person. If a document must be signed by persons holding different offices or functions and a person holds or exercises more than one of those offices or functions, that person may sign the document in more than one capacity, but only if the document indicates each capacity in which the person signs.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.315.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.50.0">1989 c 304 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.32.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 32</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.321">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.321 OFFICERS CONSIDERED ELECTED.</h1>
        <p>In the absence of an election or appointment of officers by the board or the members with voting rights, the person exercising the principal functions of the president or the treasurer is considered to have been elected to the office.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.321.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.51.0">1989 c 304 s 51</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.23.0">1990 c 488 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.33.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 33</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.331">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.331 CONTRACT RIGHTS.</h1>
        <p>The election or appointment of a person as an officer or agent does not, of itself, create contract rights. A corporation may enter into a contract with an officer or agent for a period if, in the board's judgment, the contract would be in the best interests of the corporation. The fact that the contract may be for a term longer than the terms of the directors who authorized or approved the contract does not make the contract void or voidable.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.331.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.52.0">1989 c 304 s 52</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.341">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.341 RESIGNATION; REMOVAL; VACANCIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.341.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Resignation.</span></h2>
          <p>An officer may resign by giving written notice to the corporation. The resignation is effective without acceptance when the notice is given to the corporation, unless a later effective date is named in the notice.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.341.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Removal.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as otherwise provided in the articles or bylaws, an officer may be removed, with or without cause, by a resolution adopted by the board or by the members with voting rights, whichever elected or appointed the officer. An officer appointed by the president may also be removed at any time, with or without cause, by the president. To the extent authorized in the articles, the bylaws, or a resolution approved by the affirmative vote of a majority of the directors present, the president of a corporation may remove an officer elected or appointed by the board, other than the treasurer. A removal as described in this subdivision is without prejudice to contractual rights of the officer.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.341.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Vacancy.</span></h2>
          <p>A vacancy in an office because of death, resignation, removal, disqualification, or other cause may, or in the case of a vacancy in the office of president or treasurer must, be filled for the unexpired part of the term in the manner provided in the articles or bylaws, or as determined by the board or under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.321">317A.321</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.341.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.53.0">1989 c 304 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.24.0">1990 c 488 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/503/#laws.0.9.0">1992 c 503 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.34.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.351">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.351 DELEGATION.</h1>
        <p>Unless prohibited by the articles or bylaws or by a resolution adopted by the board, an officer may, without the approval of the board, delegate some or all the duties and powers of an office to other persons. An officer who delegates the duties or powers of an office remains subject to the standard of conduct for an officer with respect to the discharge of the delegated duties and powers.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.351.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.54.0">1989 c 304 s 54</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.361">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.361 STANDARD OF CONDUCT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.361.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Standard; liability.</span></h2>
          <p>An officer shall discharge the duties of an office in good faith, in a manner the officer reasonably believes to be in the best interests of the corporation, and with the care an ordinarily prudent person in a like position would exercise under similar circumstances. A person exercising the principal functions of an office or to whom some or all of the duties and powers of an office are delegated under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.351">317A.351</a> is considered an officer for purposes of this section and sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.467">317A.467</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.521">317A.521</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.361.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Not considered trustee.</span></h2>
          <p>An officer is not considered to be a trustee with respect to the corporation or with respect to property held or administered by the corporation, including without limit, property that may be subject to restrictions imposed by the donor or transferor of the property.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.361.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.55.0">1989 c 304 s 55</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">MEMBERS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.401">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.401 MEMBERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.401.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Existence.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A corporation may have one or more classes of members or may have no members. In the absence of a provision in its articles or bylaws providing for members, a corporation has no members.</p>
          <p>(b) If a corporation has no members, an action for which there is no specific provision of this chapter applicable to a corporation without members and that would otherwise require approval of the members requires only the approval of the board.</p>
          <p>(c) A reference in this chapter to a corporation that has no members includes a corporation in which the directors are the only members.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.401.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Admission.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may admit any person as a member. The articles or bylaws may establish criteria or procedures for admission. A person may not be admitted as a member without the person's express or implied consent. For purposes of this subdivision, consent includes, but is not limited to, acceptance of membership benefits knowing that the benefits are available only to members, or taking some other affirmative action that confers membership benefits. If the articles or bylaws provide that a person who contributes to the corporation is a member, a contribution is consent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.401.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Consideration.</span></h2>
          <p>Except as provided in its articles or bylaws, a corporation may admit members for no consideration or for consideration as is determined by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.401.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Rights.</span></h2>
          <p>Members are of one class unless the articles establish, or authorize the bylaws to establish, more than one class. Members are entitled to vote and have equal rights and preferences except to the extent that the articles or bylaws have fixed or limited the rights and preferences of members or different classes of members or provide for nonvoting members. The articles or bylaws may fix the term of membership.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.401.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.56.0">1989 c 304 s 56</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.25.0">1990 c 488 s 25</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.403">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.403 MEMBERSHIP CERTIFICATES.</h1>
        <p>A corporation may issue certificates showing membership in the corporation. In lieu of a membership certificate, a corporation may issue preferred or common stock. Stock may be issued upon the terms and conditions that the board considers appropriate.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.403.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.57.0">1989 c 304 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.26.0">1990 c 488 s 26</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.405">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.405 TRANSFER OF MEMBERSHIP.</h1>
        <p>(a) Except as provided in the articles or bylaws, a member of a corporation may not transfer a membership or a right arising from it.</p>
        <p>(b) Where transfer rights have been provided, a restriction on them is not binding with respect to a member holding a membership issued before the adoption of the restriction unless the restriction is approved by the members and the affected member.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.405.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.58.0">1989 c 304 s 58</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.407">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.407 LIABILITY OF MEMBERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.407.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Third parties.</span></h2>
          <p>A member of a corporation is not, as such, personally liable for the acts, debts, liabilities, or obligations of the corporation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.407.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Dues, assessments, or fees.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When authority to do so is conferred by the articles or bylaws and subject to any limitations, a corporation may levy dues, assessments, or fees upon its members. The dues, assessments, or fees may be imposed upon all classes of members alike or differently upon different classes of members. Members of one or more classes may be exempted.</p>
          <p>(b) Articles or bylaws may:</p>
          <p>(1) fix the amount of the levy and the method of collection of dues, assessments, or fees; or</p>
          <p>(2) authorize the directors to fix the amount from time to time and determine the methods of collection.</p>
          <p>(c) Articles or bylaws may provide for:</p>
          <p>(1) enforcement or collection of dues, assessments, or fees;</p>
          <p>(2) cancellation of membership, on reasonable notice, for nonpayment of dues, assessments, or fees; or</p>
          <p>(3) reinstatement of membership.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.407.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.59.0">1989 c 304 s 59</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.409">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.409 RESIGNATION.</h1>
        <p>A member may resign at any time. The resignation of a member does not relieve the member from any obligations the member may have to the corporation for dues, assessments, or fees or charges for goods or services.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.409.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.60.0">1989 c 304 s 60</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.411">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.411 TERMINATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.411.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Fair and reasonable procedure required.</span></h2>
          <p>A member may not be expelled or suspended, and a membership may not be terminated or suspended except pursuant to a procedure that is fair and reasonable and is carried out in good faith. This section does not apply to the termination of a membership at the end of a fixed term.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.411.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Standards.</span></h2>
          <p>A procedure is fair and reasonable when it is fair and reasonable taking into consideration all of the relevant facts and circumstances. In addition, a procedure is fair and reasonable if it provides:</p>
          <p>(1) not less than 15 days' prior written notice of the expulsion, suspension, or termination, and the reasons for it; and</p>
          <p>(2) an opportunity for the member to be heard, orally or in writing, not less than five days before the effective date of the expulsion, suspension, or termination by a person authorized to decide that the proposed expulsion, termination, or suspension not take place.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.411.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Time limit to challenge.</span></h2>
          <p>A proceeding challenging an expulsion, suspension, or termination, including a proceeding in which defective notice is alleged, must be begun within one year after the effective date of the expulsion, suspension, or termination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.411.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Member liability.</span></h2>
          <p>The expulsion, suspension, or termination of a member does not relieve the member from obligations the member may have to the corporation for dues, assessments, or fees or charges for goods or services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.411.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.61.0">1989 c 304 s 61</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.413">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.413 PURCHASE OF MEMBERSHIPS.</h1>
        <p>If authorized in its articles or bylaws, a corporation may buy the membership of a member who resigns or whose membership is terminated for the amount and pursuant to the conditions in the articles or bylaws.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.413.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.62.0">1989 c 304 s 62</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.415">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.415 DELEGATES.</h1>
        <p>A corporation may provide in its articles or bylaws for delegates having some or all the authority of members. The articles or bylaws may set forth provisions relating to:</p>
        <p>(1) the characteristics, qualifications, rights, limitations, and obligations of the delegates, including their selection and removal;</p>
        <p>(2) calling, noticing, holding, and conducting meetings of delegates; and</p>
        <p>(3) carrying on corporate activities during and between meetings of delegates.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.415.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.63.0">1989 c 304 s 63</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.431">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.431 ANNUAL MEETINGS OF MEMBERS WITH VOTING RIGHTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.431.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Frequency.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless the articles or bylaws provide otherwise, a corporation with members with voting rights shall hold at least an annual meeting of members with voting rights.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.431.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Demand by members.</span></h2>
          <p>If an annual meeting of members with voting rights has not been held during the preceding 15 months, at least 50 members with voting rights or ten percent of the members with voting rights, whichever is less, may demand an annual meeting of members by written notice of demand given to the president or the treasurer of the corporation. Within 30 days after receipt of the demand, the board shall cause a meeting of members to be called and held at the expense of the corporation on notice no later than 90 days after receipt of the demand. If the board fails to cause a meeting to be called and held as required by this subdivision, the members with voting rights making the demand may call the meeting at the expense of the corporation by giving notice as required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.435">317A.435</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.431.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Time; place.</span></h2>
          <p>An annual meeting of members must be held at the time and place stated in or fixed in accordance with the articles or bylaws. If a place is not stated or if a demand for a meeting is made under subdivision 2, the meeting must be held in the county where the corporation's registered office is located. To the extent authorized in articles or bylaws, the board of directors may determine that an annual meeting of the members shall be held solely by means of remote communication in accordance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.450#stat.317A.450.2">317A.450, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.431.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Elections; business.</span></h2>
          <p>At an annual meeting of members:</p>
          <p>(1) there must be an election of successors for directors elected by members and whose terms have expired or whose terms expire at an annual meeting;</p>
          <p>(2) there must be a report on the activities and financial condition of the corporation; and</p>
          <p>(3) the members shall consider and act upon other matters as may be raised consistent with the notice of meeting requirements.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.431.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Effect of failure to hold meeting.</span></h2>
          <p>The failure to hold a meeting in accordance with a corporation's articles or bylaws does not affect the validity of a corporate action.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.431.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.64.0">1989 c 304 s 64</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.27.0">1990 c 488 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/503/#laws.0.10.0">1992 c 503 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/311/#laws.3.8.0">2002 c 311 art 3 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.19.0">2017 c 17 s 19</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.433">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.433 SPECIAL MEETINGS OF MEMBERS WITH VOTING RIGHTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.433.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Who may call.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation with members with voting rights shall hold a special meeting of members:</p>
          <p>(1) on call of its board or persons authorized to do so by the articles or bylaws; or</p>
          <p>(2) if at least 50 members with voting rights or ten percent of the members with voting rights, whichever is less, sign, date, and deliver to the president or the treasurer one or more written demands for the meeting describing the purpose for which it is to be held.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.433.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice.</span></h2>
