          <p>(8) except where the will or other instrument provides otherwise, and subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.671">317A.671</a>, a devise, bequest, gift, or grant contained in a will or other instrument, in trust or otherwise, made before or after the merger or consolidation has become effective, to or for any of the constituent corporations, inures to the single corporation;</p>
          <p>(9) debts, liabilities, and obligations of each constituent corporation become the debts, liabilities, and obligations of the single corporation, just as if the debts, liabilities, and obligations had been incurred or contracted by the single corporation;</p>
          <p>(10) existing claims or a pending action or proceeding by or against a constituent corporation may be prosecuted to judgment as though the merger or consolidation had not been effected, or the single corporation may be substituted for the constituent corporation;</p>
          <p>(11) the liabilities of the members, officers, directors, or similar groups or persons, however denominated, of a constituent corporation are not affected by the merger or consolidation of a constituent corporation;</p>
          <p>(12) the rights of creditors or liens upon the property of a constituent corporation are not impaired by the merger or consolidation, but the liens are limited to the property upon which they were liens immediately before the merger or consolidation;</p>
          <p>(13) the articles of the surviving corporation are considered to be amended to the extent that changes in its articles are contained in a plan of merger; and</p>
          <p>(14) in the case of a consolidation, the plan of consolidation constitutes the articles of incorporation of the new corporation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.641.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Effect on fiduciary capacity.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this subdivision, "fiduciary capacity" means the capacities of trustee, executor, administrator, personal representative, guardian, conservator, receiver, escrow agent, agent for the investment of money, attorney-in-fact, or a similar capacity.</p>
          <p>(b) Except where the will, declaration of trust, or other instrument provides otherwise, the single corporation is, without further act or deed, the successor of the constituent corporations in fiduciary capacities in which a constituent corporation was acting at the time of the merger or consolidation and is liable to the beneficiaries as fully as if the constituent corporation had continued its separate corporate existence.</p>
          <p>(c) If a constituent corporation is nominated and appointed, or has been nominated and appointed, in a fiduciary capacity in a will, declaration of trust, or other instrument, order, or judgment before or after the merger or consolidation, then even if the will or other instrument, order, or judgment does not become operative or effective until after the merger or consolidation becomes effective, every fiduciary capacity and the rights, powers, privileges, duties, discretions, and responsibilities provided for in the nomination or appointment fully vest in and are to be exercised by the single corporation, whether there are one or more successive mergers or consolidations.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.641.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.89.0">1989 c 304 s 89</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.643">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.643 CONTINUANCE OF CORPORATE AUTHORITY.</h1>
        <p>When an act or instrument is considered necessary or appropriate to evidence the vesting of property or other rights in the single corporation, the persons with authority to do so under the articles or bylaws of each constituent corporation shall do the act or execute and deliver the instrument and for this purpose, the existence of the constituent corporations and the authority of those persons is continued.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.643.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.90.0">1989 c 304 s 90</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.651">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.651 MERGER OR CONSOLIDATION WITH FOREIGN CORPORATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.651.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">When permitted.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may merge or consolidate with a foreign corporation by following the procedures set forth in this section, if the merger or consolidation is permitted by the laws of the state under which the foreign corporation is incorporated.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.651.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Laws applicable before transaction.</span></h2>
          <p>Each corporation shall comply with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.601">317A.601</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.643">317A.643</a> with respect to the merger or consolidation of corporations and each foreign corporation shall comply with the laws under which it was incorporated or by which it is governed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.651.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Surviving corporation.</span></h2>
          <p>If the single corporation will be incorporated under this chapter, it shall comply with this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.651.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Foreign surviving corporation.</span></h2>
          <p>If the single corporation will be a foreign corporation and will transact business in this state, it shall comply with the provisions of chapter 303 with respect to foreign corporations. In every case the single corporation shall file with the secretary of state:</p>
          <p>(1) an agreement that it may be served with process in this state in a proceeding for the enforcement of an obligation of a constituent corporation; and</p>
          <p>(2) an irrevocable appointment of the secretary of state as its agent to accept service of process in any proceeding and an address to which process may be forwarded.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.651.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.91.0">1989 c 304 s 91</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.661">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.661 TRANSFER OF ASSETS; REQUIRED APPROVAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.661.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote"> Approval required.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may sell, lease, transfer, dispose of, or grant a security interest in all or substantially all of its property and assets only as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.661.1a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1a.<span class="headnote">Transfer of assets in the regular course of business; approval by board.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise provided in its articles or bylaws, a corporation, by the affirmative vote of a majority of directors, may sell, lease, transfer, or dispose of all or substantially all of its property and assets in the usual and regular course of its activities upon those terms and conditions and for those considerations, which may be money, securities, or other instruments for the payment of money or other property, as the board considers expedient. Member approval is not required under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.661.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Transfer of assets not in the regular course of business.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A corporation, by the affirmative vote of a majority of all directors, may sell, lease, transfer, or dispose of all or substantially all of its property and assets, including its good will, not in the usual and regular course of its activities, upon those terms and conditions and for those considerations, which may be money, securities, or other instruments for the payment of money or other property, as the board considers expedient. If there are members with voting rights, the sale, lease, transfer, or disposition must be submitted to the members under paragraph (b). If there are no members with voting rights, member approval is not required.</p>
          <p>(b) Written notice must be given to each member with voting rights, within the time and in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.435">317A.435</a> for notice of meetings of members and, whether the meeting is a regular or special meeting, must state that a purpose of the meeting is to consider the sale, lease, transfer, or other disposition of all or substantially all of the property and assets of the corporation. The sale, lease, transfer, or disposition must be approved at a regular or special meeting of the members by the affirmative vote of the majority of the members with voting rights voting on the action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.661.2a">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2a.<span class="headnote">Grant of security interest; approval by board.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise provided in its articles or bylaws and subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.501#stat.317A.501.1">317A.501, subdivision 1</a>, a corporation may, by the affirmative vote of a majority of directors, grant a security interest in all or substantially all of its property and assets whether or not in the usual and regular course of its activities, upon those terms and conditions and for those considerations, which may be money, securities, or other instruments for the payment of money or other property, as the board considers expedient. Member approval is not required under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.661.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notice to attorney general.</span></h2>
          <p>If applicable, a corporation shall comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a> before transferring all or substantially all of its assets under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.661.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Signing of documents.</span></h2>
          <p>Confirmatory deeds, assignments, or similar instruments to evidence a sale, lease, transfer, or other disposition may be signed and delivered at any time in the name of the transferor by its current officers or, if the corporation no longer exists, by its last officers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.661.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Transferee liability.</span></h2>
          <p>The transferee is liable for the debts, obligations, and liabilities of the transferor only to the extent provided in the contract or agreement between the transferee and the transferor or to the extent provided by this chapter or other statutes of this state.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.661.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.92.0">1989 c 304 s 92</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.37.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 37</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.671">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.671 CERTAIN ASSETS NOT TO BE DIVERTED.</h1>
        <p>Except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/501B.31">501B.31</a>, when a corporation:</p>
        <p>(1) converts;</p>
        <p>(2) dissolves;</p>
        <p>(3) merges;</p>
        <p>(4) substantially changes the use or purposes for which it will use its assets;</p>
        <p>(5) consolidates;</p>
        <p>(6) transfers its assets; or</p>
        <p>(7) grants a mortgage or other security interest in its assets,</p>
        <p class="bl">assets of the corporation or a constituent corporation or converting corporation, and assets subsequently received by a single or converted corporation after a merger or consolidation, or held by a converted organization after a conversion may not be diverted from the uses and purposes for which the assets have been received and held, or from the uses and purposes expressed or intended by the original donor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.671.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.93.0">1989 c 304 s 93</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/340/#laws.2.9.0">1989 c 340 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/222/#laws.0.43.0">1997 c 222 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.10.0">2017 c 17 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.681">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.681 CONVERSION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.681.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.671">317A.671</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.689">317A.689</a>, the terms in this section have the meanings given.</p>
          <p>(b) "Organizational document" means:</p>
          <p>(1) for a domestic or foreign limited liability company, its articles or certificate of organization;</p>
          <p>(2) for a trust, its agreement of trust or declaration of trust;</p>
          <p>(3) for a domestic or foreign corporation, its articles or certificate of incorporation; and</p>
          <p>(4) for any other organization, the basic record that creates the organization.</p>
          <p>(c) "Converted organization" means the organization into which a converting organization converts pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.681">317A.681</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.689">317A.689</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) "Converting organization" means an organization that converts into another organization pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.681">317A.681</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.689">317A.689</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) "Converting corporation" means a corporation governed under this chapter.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.681.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Conversion requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>An organization other than a corporation may convert to a corporation, and a corporation may convert to another organization, pursuant to this section, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.683">317A.683</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.689">317A.689</a>, and a plan of conversion if:</p>
          <p>(1) the converting organization's governing statute authorizes the conversion;</p>
          <p>(2) the conversion is not prohibited by another law of this state or the law of the jurisdiction that enacted the converting organization's governing statute;</p>
          <p>(3) the converting organization complies with its governing statute in effecting the conversion;</p>
          <p>(4) the converting organization and the converted organization's governing statute prohibits the organization from:</p>
          <p>(i) being formed for a purpose involving pecuniary gain to its members, other than to members that are nonprofit organizations or subdivisions, units, or agencies of the United States or a state or local government; and</p>
          <p>(ii) paying dividends or other pecuniary remuneration, directly or indirectly, to its members, other than to members that are nonprofit organizations or subdivisions, units, or agencies of the United States or a state or local government; and</p>
          <p>(5) the attorney general has been notified, to the extent applicable under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a>, and any applicable waiting periods under that section have expired.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.681.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contents of plan of conversion.</span></h2>
          <p>A plan of conversion must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the name and form of the converting organization and the jurisdiction of the converting organization's governing statute before conversion;</p>
          <p>(2) the name and form of the converted organization and the jurisdiction of the converted organization's governing statute after conversion;</p>
          <p>(3) the terms and conditions of the conversion, including the manner and basis for converting interests in the converting organization into interests in the converted organization; and</p>
          <p>(4) the organizational documents of the converted organization as they are to be in effect upon completion of the conversion.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.681.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.11.0">2017 c 17 s 11</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.683">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.683 ACTION ON PLAN OF CONVERSION BY CONVERTING CORPORATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.683.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Approval.</span></h2>
          <p>A plan of conversion must be approved and adopted by a converting corporation as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.683.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Approval by board and members with voting rights.</span></h2>
          <p>When a corporation has members with voting rights, the board of directors of the corporation shall adopt a resolution by the affirmative vote of a majority of all directors approving a proposed plan of conversion and directing that the plan be submitted to a vote at a meeting of the members with voting rights. Notice of the meeting must be given to each member with voting rights, accompanied by a copy or summary of the proposed plan. Unless the articles or bylaws require a greater vote, the plan of conversion is adopted upon receiving the affirmative vote of a majority of the members with voting rights voting on the action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.683.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Approval by board.</span></h2>
          <p>When a corporation does not have members with voting rights, and unless the articles or bylaws require a greater vote, a plan of conversion is adopted at a meeting of the board of directors of the corporation upon receiving the affirmative votes of a majority of all directors. Notice of the meeting must be given, accompanied by a copy of the proposed plan of conversion.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.683.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.12.0">2017 c 17 s 12</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.685">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.685 FILINGS REQUIRED FOR CONVERSION; EFFECTIVE DATE AND TIME.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.685.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Articles of conversion.</span></h2>
          <p>After a plan of conversion is approved:</p>
          <p>(1) if the converting organization is a converting corporation, the converting corporation shall file articles of conversion with the secretary of state, which must be signed as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.011#stat.317A.011.19">317A.011, subdivision 19</a>, and must include:</p>
          <p>(i) the plan of conversion;</p>
          <p>(ii) a statement that the corporation is converting into another organization;</p>
          <p>(iii) the name and form of the converted organization and the jurisdiction of its governing statute;</p>
          <p>(iv) the time the conversion is effective under the governing statute of the converted organization;</p>
          <p>(v) a statement that the conversion was approved as required by this chapter;</p>
          <p>(vi) a statement that the conversion was approved as required by the governing statute of the converted organization;</p>
          <p>(vii) if the converted organization is a domestic organization, the organizational document of the converted organization;</p>
          <p>(viii) if the converted organization is a foreign organization not authorized to transact business in this state, the street address of an office that the secretary of state may use for the purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.689#stat.317A.689.3">317A.689, subdivision 3</a>; and</p>
          <p>(ix) if applicable, a statement that the notice to the attorney general required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a> has been given and any applicable period has expired or has been waived by the attorney general, or a statement that section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a> is not applicable; and</p>
          <p>(2) if the converting organization is not a converting corporation, the converting organization shall file articles of conversion with the secretary of state, which must be signed as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.011#stat.317A.011.19">317A.011, subdivision 19</a>, and must include:</p>
          <p>(i) articles of incorporation for the corporation into which the converting organization is converting, which must include the information required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.111">317A.111, subdivision </a>1, clauses (1) and (2);</p>
          <p>(ii) the plan of conversion;</p>
          <p>(iii) a statement that the converting organization is converting into a corporation;</p>
          <p>(iv) the name and form of the converting organization and the jurisdiction of its governing statute;</p>
          <p>(v) a statement that the conversion was approved in a manner that complied with the converting organization's governing statute; and</p>
          <p>(vi) if applicable, a statement that the notice to the attorney general required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a> has been given and any applicable waiting period has expired or has been waived by the attorney general, or a statement that section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a> is not applicable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.685.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Effective date and time of conversion.</span></h2>
          <p>A conversion becomes effective:</p>
          <p>(1) if the converted organization is a corporation, when the articles of conversion are filed with the secretary of state or on a later date or at a later time specified in the articles of conversion; or</p>
          <p>(2) if the converted organization is not a corporation, as provided by the governing statute of the converted organization.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.685.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Certificate.</span></h2>
          <p>The secretary of state shall issue to the converted organization or its legal representative a certificate of conversion and, if the converted organization is a corporation, a certificate of incorporation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.685.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.13.0">2017 c 17 s 13</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.687">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.687 ABANDONMENT.</h1>
        <p>Subject to any contractual rights, after a conversion is approved, and at any time before articles of conversion are delivered to the secretary of state for filing under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.685">317A.685</a>, a converting corporation may amend the plan or abandon the conversion:</p>
        <p>(1) as provided in the plan; or</p>
        <p>(2) except as otherwise prohibited in the plan, by the same consent as was required to approve the plan.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.687.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.14.0">2017 c 17 s 14</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.689">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.689 EFFECT OF CONVERSION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.689.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Effect on corporation; general.</span></h2>
          <p>When a conversion becomes effective, an organization that has been converted pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.681">317A.681</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.687">317A.687</a> is for all purposes the same entity that existed before the conversion, and:</p>
          <p>(1) all property owned by the converting organization remains vested in the converted organization and no assignment by operation of law or otherwise of its assets, properties, or contracts shall be deemed to have occurred;</p>
          <p>(2) all debts, obligations, or other liabilities of the converting organization continue as debts, obligations, or other liabilities of the converted organization;</p>
          <p>(3) an action or proceeding pending by or against the converting organization may be continued as if the conversion had not occurred or as actions or proceedings by or against the converted organization;</p>
          <p>(4) except as prohibited by law other than this chapter, all rights, privileges, immunities, powers, and purposes of the converting organization remain vested in the converted organization; and</p>
          <p>(5) the conversion does not dissolve a converting corporation for the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.701">317A.701</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.791">317A.791</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.689.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Effect on fiduciary capacity.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For purposes of this subdivision, "fiduciary capacity" means the capacities of a trustee, executor, administrator, personal representative, guardian, conservator, receiver, escrow agent, agent for the investment of money, attorney-in-fact, or a similar capacity.</p>
          <p>(b) Except where the will, declaration of trust, or other instrument provides otherwise, the converted organization is, without further act or deed, the successor of the converting organization in fiduciary capacities in which a corporation was acting at the time of the conversion and is liable to the beneficiaries as fully as if the corporation had continued its prior corporate existence.</p>
          <p>(c) If a converting organization or converting corporation is or has been nominated and appointed in a fiduciary capacity in a will, declaration of trust, or other instrument, order, or judgment before or after the conversion, then even if the will or other instrument, order, or judgment does not become operative or effective until after the conversion becomes effective, every fiduciary capacity and the rights, powers, privileges, duties, discretions, and responsibilities provided for in the nomination or appointment fully vest in and are to be exercised by the converting corporation, whether there are one or more successive conversions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.689.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Foreign organization.</span></h2>
          <p>A converted organization that is a foreign organization consents to the jurisdiction of the courts of this state to enforce any debt, obligation, or other liability for which the converting corporation is liable if, before the conversion, the converting corporation was subject to suit in this state on the debt, obligation, or other liability. A converted organization that is a foreign organization and not authorized to transact business in this state appoints the secretary of state as its agent for service of process for purposes of enforcing a debt, obligation, or other liability under this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.689.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.15.0">2017 c 17 s 15</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">DISSOLUTION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.701">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.701 METHODS OF DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <p>(a) Subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a>, a corporation may be dissolved:</p>
        <p>(1) by the incorporators under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.711">317A.711</a>;</p>
        <p>(2) by the board and members with voting rights under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.721">317A.721</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.733">317A.733</a>; or</p>
        <p>(3) by order of a court under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.741">317A.741</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.765">317A.765</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) A corporation also may be dissolved by the secretary of state under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.827">317A.827</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.701.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.94.0">1989 c 304 s 94</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.711">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.711 VOLUNTARY DISSOLUTION BY INCORPORATORS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.711.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Manner.</span></h2>
          <p>If the first board of directors has not been named in the articles, designated or appointed pursuant to the articles, or elected under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.171">317A.171</a>, a corporation may be dissolved by the incorporators as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.711.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Articles of dissolution.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A majority of the incorporators shall sign articles of dissolution containing:</p>
          <p>(1) the name of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(2) the date of incorporation;</p>
          <p>(3) a statement that the first board of directors has not been named in the articles, designated or appointed pursuant to the articles, or elected at an organizational meeting;</p>
          <p>(4) a statement that no debts remain unpaid; and</p>
          <p>(5) a statement that notice to the attorney general required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a> has been given and the waiting period has expired or has been waived by the attorney general or a statement that section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a> is not applicable.</p>
          <p>(b) The articles of dissolution must be filed with the secretary of state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.711.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Effective date.</span></h2>
          <p>When the articles of dissolution have been filed with the secretary of state, the corporation is dissolved.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.711.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Certificate.</span></h2>
          <p>The secretary of state shall issue to the dissolved corporation a certificate of dissolution that contains:</p>
          <p>(1) the name of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(2) the date the articles of dissolution were filed with the secretary of state; and</p>
          <p>(3) a statement that the corporation is dissolved.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.711.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.95.0">1989 c 304 s 95</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.33.0">1990 c 488 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/106/#laws.0.13.0">2011 c 106 s 13</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.721">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.721 VOLUNTARY DISSOLUTION BY BOARD AND MEMBERS WITH VOTING RIGHTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.721.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Approval required.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may be dissolved by the board and members with voting rights as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.721.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Approval by board; plan of dissolution.</span></h2>
          <p>The board shall adopt a resolution proposing dissolution of the corporation by the affirmative vote of a majority of all directors. The resolution must include a plan of dissolution that states to whom the assets owned or held by the corporation will be distributed after creditors are paid. The plan must comply with the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.735">317A.735</a>. If the board will have discretion in distributing assets, the plan must state that the assets will be distributed to persons the board subsequently identifies. If there are members with voting rights, the resolution and plan of dissolution must be submitted to the members under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.721.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Approval by members with voting rights.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Written notice must be given to each member with voting rights, within the time and in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.435">317A.435</a> for notice of meetings of members and, whether the meeting is a regular or a special meeting, must state that a purpose of the meeting is to consider dissolving the corporation.</p>
          <p>(b) The proposed dissolution must be submitted for approval at a meeting of members. If the proposed dissolution is approved by the members with voting rights, the dissolution must be started.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.721.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.96.0">1989 c 304 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2010/0/250/#laws.1.38.0">2010 c 250 art 1 s 38</a>,39</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.723">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.723 FILING NOTICE OF INTENT TO DISSOLVE; EFFECT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.723.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Contents.</span></h2>
          <p>If dissolution of the corporation is approved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.721">317A.721</a>, the corporation shall file with the secretary of state a notice of intent to dissolve. The notice must contain:</p>
          <p>(1) the name of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(2) the date and place of the meeting at which the resolution was approved by the board under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.721#stat.317A.721.2">317A.721, subdivision 2</a>, and by the members under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.721#stat.317A.721.3">317A.721, subdivision 3</a>, if applicable; and</p>
          <p>(3) a statement that the requisite approval of the directors and members was received.</p>
          <p>If applicable, the corporation also shall notify the attorney general under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.723.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Winding up.</span></h2>
          <p>When the notice of intent to dissolve has been filed with the secretary of state and subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.731">317A.731</a>, the corporation may not carry on its activities, except to the extent necessary for the winding up of the corporation. The board and members with voting rights have the right to revoke the dissolution proceedings under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.731">317A.731</a> and the members with voting rights have the right to remove directors or fill vacancies on the board. The corporate existence continues to the extent necessary to wind up the affairs of the corporation until the dissolution proceedings are revoked or articles of dissolution are filed with the secretary of state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.723.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Remedies continued.</span></h2>
          <p>The filing with the secretary of state of a notice of intent to dissolve does not affect a remedy in favor of the corporation or a remedy against it or its directors, officers, or members in those capacities, except as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.781">317A.781</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.723.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.97.0">1989 c 304 s 97</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.725">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.725 PROCEDURE IN DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.725.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Collection; payment.</span></h2>
          <p>When a notice of intent to dissolve has been filed with the secretary of state, the board, or the officers acting under the direction of the board, shall proceed as soon as possible:</p>
          <p>(1) to collect or make provision for the collection of debts due or owing to the corporation; and</p>
          <p>(2) to pay or make provision for the payment of debts, obligations, and liabilities of the corporation according to their priorities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.725.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Transfer of assets.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.661">317A.661</a>, when a notice of intent to dissolve has been filed with the secretary of state, the directors may sell, lease, transfer, or otherwise dispose of all or substantially all of the property and assets of a dissolving corporation without a vote of the members, subject to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.671">317A.671</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.725.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Distribution of assets.</span></h2>
          <p>Tangible or intangible property, including money, remaining after the discharge of the debts, obligations, and liabilities of the corporation must be distributed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.735">317A.735</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.725.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.98.0">1989 c 304 s 98</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.727">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.727 NOTICE TO CREDITORS AND CLAIMANTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.727.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">When permitted; how given.</span></h2>
          <p>When a notice of intent to dissolve has been filed with the secretary of state and the attorney general, if applicable, the corporation may give notice of the filing to each creditor of and claimant against the corporation known or unknown, present or future, and contingent or noncontingent. If notice to creditors and claimants is given, it must be given by publishing the notice once each week for four successive weeks in a legal newspaper in the county where the registered office of the corporation is located and by giving written notice to known creditors and claimants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.011#stat.317A.011.14">317A.011, subdivision 14</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.727.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents.</span></h2>
          <p>The notice to creditors and claimants must contain:</p>
          <p>(1) a statement that the corporation is in the process of dissolving;</p>
          <p>(2) a statement that the corporation has filed a notice of intent to dissolve with the secretary of state;</p>
          <p>(3) the date of filing the notice of intent to dissolve;</p>
          <p>(4) the address of the office to which written claims against the corporation must be presented; and</p>
          <p>(5) the date by which the claims must be received, which is the later of 90 days after published notice or, with respect to a particular known creditor or claimant, 90 days after the date on which written notice is given to that creditor or claimant. Published notice is considered given on the date of first publication for determining this date.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.727.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.99.0">1989 c 304 s 99</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.729">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.729 CLAIMS IN DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <p>If the corporation gives notice to creditors and claimants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.727">317A.727</a>:</p>
        <p>(1) the corporation has 30 days from the receipt of each claim filed according to the procedures set forth by the corporation on or before the date set forth in the notice to accept or reject the claim by giving written notice to the person submitting it, a claim not expressly rejected in this manner is considered accepted; and</p>
        <p>(2) a creditor or claimant to whom notice is given and whose claim is rejected by the corporation has 60 days from the date of rejection, or 180 days from the date the corporation filed the notice of intent to dissolve with the secretary of state, or 90 days after the date on which notice was given to the creditor or claimant, whichever is longer, to pursue other remedies with respect to the claim.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.729.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.100.0">1989 c 304 s 100</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.730">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.730 STATUTE OF LIMITATIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.730.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Corporations that give notice.</span></h2>
          <p>If the corporation gives notice to creditors and claimants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.727">317A.727</a>:</p>
          <p>(1) the claim of a creditor or claimant to whom notice is given who fails to file a claim according to the procedures set forth by the corporation on or before the date set forth in the notice is subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.781">317A.781</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) the claim of a creditor or claimant that is rejected by the corporation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.729">317A.729</a> is subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.781">317A.781</a> if the creditor or claimant does not begin legal, administrative, or arbitration proceedings with respect to the claim during the period set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.729">317A.729</a>, clause (2).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.730.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Other corporations.</span></h2>
          <p>If the corporation does not give notice to creditors and claimants under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.727">317A.727</a>, the claim of a creditor or claimant who does not begin legal, administrative, or arbitration proceedings concerning the claim within two years after the date of filing the notice of intent to dissolve is subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.781">317A.781</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.730.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.101.0">1989 c 304 s 101</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.731">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.731 REVOCATION OF DISSOLUTION PROCEEDINGS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.731.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Generally.</span></h2>
          <p>Dissolution proceedings begun under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.721">317A.721</a> may be revoked before the articles of dissolution are filed as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.731.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Revocation by board.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may adopt a resolution revoking the proposed dissolution by the affirmative vote of a majority of all directors. If there are members with voting rights, the resolution must be submitted to the members under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.731.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Approval by members with voting rights.</span></h2>
          <p>Written notice must be given to the members with voting rights within the time and in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.435">317A.435</a> for notice of meetings of members and must state that a purpose of the meeting is to consider the advisability of revoking the dissolution proceedings. The proposed revocation must be submitted to the members at the meeting. If the proposed revocation is approved by the members with voting rights, the dissolution proceedings are revoked.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.731.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Effective date; effect.</span></h2>
          <p>Revocation of dissolution proceedings is effective when a notice of revocation is filed with the secretary of state. After the notice is filed, the corporation may resume business. If notice to the attorney general has been given under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a>, the notice of revocation also must be given to the attorney general on or before the time that it is filed with the secretary of state.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.731.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.102.0">1989 c 304 s 102</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.733">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.733 ARTICLES OF DISSOLUTION; CERTIFICATE OF DISSOLUTION; EFFECT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.733.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Articles; when filed.</span></h2>
          <p>Articles of dissolution for a corporation dissolving under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.721">317A.721</a> must be filed with the secretary of state after compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a>, if applicable, and:</p>
          <p>(1) the payment of claims of known creditors and claimants has been made or provided for;</p>
          <p>(2) if the corporation has given notice to creditors and claimants in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.727">317A.727</a>: (i) the 90-day period in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.727#stat.317A.727.2">317A.727, subdivision 2</a>, clause (5), has expired and the payment of claims of the creditors and claimants filing a claim within that period has been made or provided for; or (ii) the longer of the periods described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.729">317A.729</a>, clause (2), has expired; or, in all other cases;</p>
          <p>(3) the two-year period described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.730">317A.730</a> has expired.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.733.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Contents of articles.</span></h2>
          <p>The articles of dissolution must state:</p>
          <p>(a)(1) whether notice has been given to the creditors and claimants of the corporation in the manner provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.727">317A.727</a> and, if notice has been given, the last date on which the notice was given and: (i) that the payment of the creditors and claimants filing a claim within the 90-day period set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.727#stat.317A.727.2">317A.727, subdivision 2</a>, clause (5), has been made or provided for; or (ii) the date on which the longer of the periods described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.729">317A.729</a>, clause (2), expired; or</p>
          <p>(2) if notice was not given and articles of dissolution are being filed under subdivision 1, clause (1), that the debts, obligations, and liabilities of the corporation have been paid and discharged or that adequate provisions have been made for them;</p>
          <p>(b) that the remaining assets of the corporation have been distributed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.735">317A.735</a> or that adequate provision has been made for the distribution;</p>
          <p>(c) that there are no pending legal, administrative, or arbitration proceedings by or against the corporation, or that adequate provision has been made for the satisfaction of a judgment, order, or decree that may be entered against it in a pending proceeding; and</p>
          <p>(d) if applicable, that notice to the attorney general required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811">317A.811</a> has been given and the waiting period has expired or has been waived by the attorney general.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.733.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Effective date.</span></h2>
          <p>When the articles of dissolution have been filed with the secretary of state, or on a later date or a later time each within 30 days after filing if the articles of dissolution so provide, the corporation is dissolved.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.733.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Certificate.</span></h2>
          <p>The secretary of state shall issue to the dissolved corporation a certificate of dissolution that contains:</p>
          <p>(1) the name of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(2) the date the dissolution was filed with the secretary of state; and</p>
          <p>(3) a statement that the corporation is dissolved at the effective date and time of the dissolution.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.733.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.103.0">1989 c 304 s 103</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/186/#laws.0.65.0">1995 c 186 s 65</a>,66; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/311/#laws.3.13.0">2002 c 311 art 3 s 13</a>,14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/106/#laws.0.14.0">2011 c 106 s 14</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.735">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.735 DISTRIBUTION OF ASSETS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.735.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General.</span></h2>
          <p>In performing their duties under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.725">317A.725</a>, the board, or the officers acting under the direction of the board, shall distribute the assets of the corporation in the following order of priority:</p>
          <p>(1) distribution of assets received and held for a special use or purpose under subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(2) payment of costs and expenses of the dissolution proceedings, including attorney fees and disbursements;</p>
          <p>(3) payment of debts, obligations, and liabilities of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(4) distribution of assets pursuant to articles or bylaws of the dissolving corporation or the rules or canons of another organization under subdivision 3; and</p>
          <p>(5) distribution of remaining assets under subdivision 4.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.735.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Special use or purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>Assets of the corporation may not be diverted from the uses and purposes for which the assets have been received and held, or from the uses and purposes expressed or intended by the original donor.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.735.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Articles, bylaws, or another organization.</span></h2>
          <p>Where the articles or bylaws of the dissolving corporation, or the rules or canons of another organization by which the dissolving corporation is bound, provide for a particular distribution of the assets of the dissolving corporation, the assets must be distributed accordingly.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.735.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Remainder.</span></h2>
          <p>The distribution of assets held for or devoted to a charitable or public use or purpose is subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/501B.31">501B.31</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.735.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.104.0">1989 c 304 s 104</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/340/#laws.2.10.0">1989 c 340 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.34.0">1990 c 488 s 34</a>,35</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.741">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.741 SUPERVISED VOLUNTARY DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <p>After the notice of intent to dissolve has been filed with the secretary of state and before a certificate of dissolution has been issued, the corporation, the attorney general, or, for good cause, a creditor or at least 50 members with voting rights or ten percent of the members with voting rights, whichever is less, may apply to a court within the county in which the registered office of the corporation is located to have the dissolution conducted or continued under the supervision of the court under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.751">317A.751</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.765">317A.765</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.741.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.105.0">1989 c 304 s 105</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.751">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.751 JUDICIAL INTERVENTION; EQUITABLE REMEDIES OR DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.751.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General; when permitted.</span></h2>
          <p>A court may grant equitable relief it considers just and reasonable in the circumstances or may dissolve a corporation and liquidate its assets and business as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.751.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Supervised voluntary dissolution.</span></h2>
          <p>A court may grant equitable relief in a supervised voluntary dissolution under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.741">317A.741</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.751.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Action by director or members with voting rights.</span></h2>
          <p>A court may grant equitable relief in an action by a director or at least 50 members with voting rights or ten percent of the members with voting rights, whichever is less, when it is established that:</p>
          <p>(1) the directors or the persons having the authority otherwise vested in the board are deadlocked in the management of the corporate affairs, the members cannot break the deadlock, and the corporation or the parties have not provided for a procedure to resolve the dispute;</p>
          <p>(2) the directors or those in control of the corporation have acted fraudulently, illegally, or in a manner unfairly prejudicial toward one or more members in their capacities as members, directors, or officers;</p>
          <p>(3) the members of the corporation are so divided in voting power that, for a period that includes the time when two consecutive regular meetings were held, they have failed to elect successors to directors whose terms have expired or would have expired upon the election and qualification of their successors;</p>
          <p>(4) the corporate assets are being misapplied or wasted; or</p>
          <p>(5) the period of duration as provided in the articles has expired.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.751.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Action by creditor.</span></h2>
          <p>A court may grant equitable relief in an action by a creditor when:</p>
          <p>(1) the claim of the creditor has been reduced to judgment and an execution on it has been returned unsatisfied; or</p>
          <p>(2) the corporation has admitted in writing that the claim of the creditor is due and owing and it is established that the corporation cannot pay its debts in the ordinary course of its activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.751.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Action by attorney general.</span></h2>
          <p>A court may grant equitable relief in an action by the attorney general when it is established that:</p>
          <p>(1) the articles and certificate of incorporation were obtained through fraud;</p>
          <p>(2) the corporation should not have been formed under this chapter;</p>
          <p>(3) the corporation failed to comply with the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.021">317A.021</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.155">317A.155</a> essential to incorporation under or election to become governed by this chapter;</p>
          <p>(4) the corporation has flagrantly violated a provision of this chapter, has violated a provision of this chapter more than once, or has violated more than one provision of this chapter;</p>
          <p>(5) the corporation has engaged in an unauthorized act, contract, conveyance, or transfer or has exceeded its powers;</p>
          <p>(6) the corporation has acted, or failed to act, in a manner that constitutes surrender or abandonment of the corporate purpose, franchise, privileges, or enterprise;</p>
          <p>(7) the corporation has liabilities and obligations exceeding the corporate assets;</p>
          <p>(8) the period of corporate existence has ended without extension;</p>
          <p>(9) the corporation has failed for a period of 90 days to pay fees, charges, or penalties required by this chapter;</p>
          <p>(10) the corporation has failed for a period of 30 days after changing its registered office to file with the secretary of state a statement of the change;</p>
          <p>(11) the corporation has answered falsely or failed to answer a reasonable written interrogatory from the secretary of state, the attorney general, the commissioner of human services, commissioner of commerce, or commissioner of revenue, to the corporation, its officers, or directors;</p>
          <p>(12) the corporation has solicited property and has failed to use it for the purpose solicited; or</p>
          <p>(13) the corporation has fraudulently used or solicited property.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.751.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Condition of corporation.</span></h2>
          <p>In determining whether to order equitable relief or dissolution under this section, the court shall consider the financial condition of the corporation but may not refuse to order equitable relief or dissolution solely on the ground that the corporation is solvent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.751.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Dissolution as remedy.</span></h2>
          <p>In deciding whether to order dissolution, the court shall consider whether lesser relief suggested by one or more parties, such as any form of equitable relief or a partial liquidation, would be adequate to permanently relieve the circumstances established under subdivision 3, 4, or 5. Lesser relief may be ordered if it would be appropriate under the facts and circumstances of the case.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.751.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>If the court finds that a party to a proceeding brought under this section has acted arbitrarily, vexatiously, or otherwise not in good faith, it may award reasonable expenses, including attorneys fees and disbursements, to any of the other parties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.751.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Venue; parties.</span></h2>
          <p>Proceedings under this section must be brought in a court within the county in which the registered office of the corporation is located. It is not necessary to make members parties to the action or proceeding unless relief is sought against them personally.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.751.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.106.0">1989 c 304 s 106</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/503/#laws.0.13.0">1992 c 503 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/106/#laws.0.15.0">2011 c 106 s 15</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.753">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.753 PROCEDURE IN INVOLUNTARY OR SUPERVISED VOLUNTARY DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.753.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Action before hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>In dissolution proceedings the court may issue injunctions, appoint receivers with powers and duties the court directs, take other actions required to preserve the corporate assets wherever located, and carry on the business of the corporation until a full hearing can be held.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.753.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Notice to attorney general; intervention.</span></h2>
          <p>When a proceeding involving a corporation described in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.811#stat.317A.811.1">317A.811, subdivision 1</a>, is begun, the court shall order that a copy of the petition be served on the attorney general. In all proceedings under this section, the attorney general has a right to participate as a party.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.753.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Action after hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>After a full hearing has been held, upon whatever notice the court directs to be given to the parties to the proceedings and to other parties in interest designated by the court, the court may appoint a receiver to collect the corporate assets. In addition to the powers set forth in chapter 576, a receiver has authority, subject to the order of the court, to continue the business of the corporation and to sell, lease, transfer, or otherwise dispose of all or any of the assets of the corporation at a public or private sale.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.753.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Discharge of obligations.</span></h2>
          <p>The assets of the corporation or the proceeds resulting from a sale, lease, transfer, or other disposition must be applied in the order of priority set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.51">576.51</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.753.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Remainder.</span></h2>
          <p>After payment of the expenses of receivership and claims of creditors duly proved, the remaining assets, if any, must be distributed as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.735">317A.735</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.753.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.107.0">1989 c 304 s 107</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/143/#laws.3.17.0">2012 c 143 art 3 s 17</a>,18</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.755">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.755 QUALIFICATIONS OF RECEIVERS; POWERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.755.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Qualifications.</span></h2>
          <p>Any person qualified under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.26">576.26</a> may be appointed as a receiver. A receiver shall give bond as required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.27">576.27</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.755.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Powers.</span></h2>
          <p>A receiver may sue and defend all actions as receiver of the corporation. The court appointing the receiver has exclusive jurisdiction over the corporation, the receiver, and all receivership property pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.23">576.23</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.755.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.108.0">1989 c 304 s 108</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/143/#laws.3.19.0">2012 c 143 art 3 s 19</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.759">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.759 FILING CLAIMS IN PROCEEDINGS TO DISSOLVE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.759.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Filing may be required.</span></h2>
          <p>In a proceeding under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.751">317A.751</a> to dissolve a corporation, the court may require creditors and claimants of the corporation to file their claims pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.49">576.49</a>. The receiver or any party in interest may object to any claim pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/576.50">576.50</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.759.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/143/#laws.3.39.0">2012 c 143 art 3 s 39</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.759.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.109.0">1989 c 304 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/143/#laws.3.20.0">2012 c 143 art 3 s 20</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.763">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.763 DECREE OF DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.763.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Procedure; when entered.</span></h2>
          <p>In an involuntary or supervised voluntary dissolution, the court shall provide for the discharge of obligations and the distribution of the assets as set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.753#stat.317A.753.4">317A.753, subdivision 4</a>, and shall enter a decree dissolving the corporation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.763.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Effective date.</span></h2>
          <p>When the decree dissolving the corporation has been entered, the corporation is dissolved.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.763.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.110.0">1989 c 304 s 110</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.765">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.765 FILING DECREE.</h1>
        <p>After the court enters a decree dissolving a corporation, the court administrator shall cause a certified copy of the decree to be filed with the secretary of state. The secretary of state may not charge a fee for filing the decree.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.765.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.111.0">1989 c 304 s 111</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.771">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.771 DEPOSIT WITH COMMISSIONER OF MANAGEMENT AND BUDGET OF AMOUNT DUE CERTAIN PERSONS.</h1>
        <p>Upon dissolution of a corporation, the part of the assets distributable to a person who is unknown or cannot be found, or who is under disability, if there is no person legally competent to receive it, must be reduced to money and deposited with the commissioner of management and budget. The amount deposited is appropriated to the commissioner of management and budget and must be paid over to the person, upon proof satisfactory to the commissioner of management and budget of a right to payment.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.771.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.112.0">1989 c 304 s 112</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/112/#laws.2.50.0">2003 c 112 art 2 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.781">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.781 CLAIMS BARRED; EXCEPTIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.781.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Claims barred.</span></h2>
          <p>A person who is or becomes a creditor or claimant at any time before, during, or following the conclusion of dissolution proceedings, who does not file a claim or pursue a remedy in a legal, administrative, or arbitration proceeding within the time provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.730">317A.730</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.741">317A.741</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.751">317A.751</a>, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.759">317A.759</a>, or has not begun a legal, administrative, or arbitration proceeding before the beginning of the dissolution proceedings, and a person claiming through or under the creditor or claimant, is barred from suing on that claim or otherwise realizing upon or enforcing it, except as provided in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.781.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Claims reopened.</span></h2>
          <p>Within one year after articles of dissolution have been filed with the secretary of state under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.733#stat.317A.733.1">317A.733, subdivision 1</a>, clause (1) or (2), or a decree of dissolution has been entered, a creditor or claimant who shows good cause for not having previously filed the claim may apply to a court in this state to allow a claim against the corporation to the extent of undistributed assets.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.781.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Claims permitted.</span></h2>
          <p>Debts, obligations, and liabilities incurred during dissolution proceedings must be paid or provided for by the corporation before the distribution of assets under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.735">317A.735</a>. A person to whom this kind of debt, obligation, or liability is owed but not paid may pursue any remedy against the officers or directors of the corporation before the expiration of the applicable statute of limitations. This subdivision does not apply to dissolution under the supervision or order of a court.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.781.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.113.0">1989 c 304 s 113</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.783">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.783 RIGHT TO SUE OR DEFEND AFTER DISSOLUTION.</h1>
        <p>After a corporation has been dissolved, its former officers, directors, or members with voting rights may assert or defend, in the name of the corporation, a claim by or against the corporation.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.783.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.114.0">1989 c 304 s 114</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.791">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.791 OMITTED ASSETS.</h1>
        <p>Title to assets remaining after payment of the debts, obligations, or liabilities and after distributions may be transferred by a court in this state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.791.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.115.0">1989 c 304 s 115</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.317A.801"><b>317A.801</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/106/#laws.0.27.0">2011 c 106 s 27</a>]</div>
      <div class="sr" id="stat.317A.805"><b>317A.805</b> [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/106/#laws.0.27.0">2011 c 106 s 27</a>]</div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ATTORNEY GENERAL</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.811">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.811 NOTICE TO ATTORNEY GENERAL; WAITING PERIOD.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.811.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">When required.</span></h2>
