          <p>(a) Except as provided in subdivision 6, the following corporations shall notify the attorney general of their intent to dissolve, merge, consolidate, or convert, or to transfer all or substantially all of their assets:</p>
          <p>(1) a corporation that holds assets for a charitable purpose as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/501B.35#stat.501B.35.2">501B.35, subdivision 2</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) a corporation that is exempt under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, or any successor section; or</p>
          <p>(3) effective July 1, 2025, a nonprofit health coverage entity as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145D.30">145D.30</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) The notice must include:</p>
          <p>(1) the purpose of the corporation that is giving the notice;</p>
          <p>(2) a list of assets owned or held by the corporation for charitable purposes;</p>
          <p>(3) a description of restricted assets and purposes for which the assets were received;</p>
          <p>(4) a description of debts, obligations, and liabilities of the corporation;</p>
          <p>(5) a description of tangible assets being converted to cash and the manner in which they will be sold;</p>
          <p>(6) anticipated expenses of the transaction, including attorney fees;</p>
          <p>(7) a list of persons to whom assets will be transferred, if known, or the name of the converted organization;</p>
          <p>(8) the purposes of persons receiving the assets or of the converted organization; and</p>
          <p>(9) the terms, conditions, or restrictions, if any, to be imposed on the transferred or converted assets.</p>
          <p>The notice must be signed on behalf of the corporation by an authorized person.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.811.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Restriction on transfers.</span></h2>
          <p>Subject to subdivision 3, a corporation described in subdivision 1 may not transfer or convey assets as part of a dissolution, merger, consolidation, or transfer of assets under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.661">317A.661</a>, and it may not convert until 45 days after it has given written notice to the attorney general, unless the attorney general waives all or part of the waiting period.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.811.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Extension of waiting period.</span></h2>
          <p>The attorney general may extend the waiting period under subdivision 2 for one additional 30-day period by notifying the corporation in writing of the extension. The attorney general shall notify the secretary of state if the waiting period is extended.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.811.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Notice after transfer.</span></h2>
          <p>When all or substantially all of the assets of a corporation described in subdivision 1 have been transferred or conveyed following expiration or waiver of the waiting period, the board shall deliver to the attorney general a list of persons to whom the assets were transferred or conveyed. The list must include the addresses of each person who received assets and show what assets the person received.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.811.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Effect.</span></h2>
          <p>Failure of the attorney general to take an action with respect to a transaction under this section does not constitute approval of the transaction and does not prevent the attorney general from taking other action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.811.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Exception.</span></h2>
          <p>Subdivisions 1 to 4 do not apply to a merger with, consolidation into, conversion into, or transfer of assets to an organization exempt under section 501(c)(3) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, or any successor section. A corporation that is exempt under this subdivision shall send a copy of the certificate of merger, certificate of consolidation and incorporation, or certificate of conversion to the attorney general.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.811.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.118.0">1989 c 304 s 118</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.36.0">1990 c 488 s 36</a>-38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/0/17/#laws.0.16.0">2017 c 17 s 16</a>-18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.57.68.0">2024 c 127 art 57 s 68</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.813">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.813 REMEDIAL POWERS OF ATTORNEY GENERAL.</h1>
        <p>The attorney general has the powers in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/8.31">8.31</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/501B.40">501B.40</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/501B.41">501B.41</a>, to supervise and investigate corporations under this chapter and to bring proceedings to secure compliance.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.813.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.119.0">1989 c 304 s 119</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/340/#laws.2.11.0">1989 c 340 art 2 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">CORPORATE REGISTRATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.821">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.821 INITIAL CORPORATE REGISTRATION WITH SECRETARY OF STATE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.821.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Notice from secretary of state; registration required.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Before February 1, 1990, the secretary of state shall mail a corporate registration form by first-class mail to each corporation at its last registered office address listed in the records of the secretary of state. The form must include the exact legal corporate name and registered office address currently on file with the secretary of state.</p>
          <p>(b) A corporation that is subject to chapter 317 shall file an initial corporate registration with the secretary of state between January 1, 1990, and December 31, 1990. The registration must include the exact legal corporate name and registered office address of the corporation and must be signed by an authorized person. If the current registered office address listed in the records of the secretary of state is not in compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.011#stat.317A.011.2">317A.011, subdivision 2</a>, or if the corporation has changed its registered office address to an address other than that listed with the secretary of state, the corporation shall list a new registered office address that complies with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.011#stat.317A.011.2">317A.011, subdivision 2</a>, on the registration form. A fee of $35 must be paid for filing the registered office address change, provided that a fee may not be charged if the registered office address is being changed only because of failure to comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.011#stat.317A.011.2">317A.011, subdivision 2</a>. The new registered office address must have been approved by the board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.821.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Loss of good standing.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation that does not file the initial corporate registration required under subdivision 1 with the secretary of state on or before December 31, 1990, loses its good standing. To regain its good standing, the corporation must file the initial corporate registration. If, as a part of the initial corporate registration process the corporation needs to bring its registered office address into compliance with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.011#stat.317A.011.2">317A.011, subdivision 2</a>, the fees stated in subdivision 1, paragraph (b), apply.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.821.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Dissolution; extension.</span></h2>
          <p>If a corporation fails to regain its good standing under subdivision 2 on or before December 31, 1997, the corporation is dissolved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.827">317A.827</a>. After December 31, 1997, the corporate existence of a corporation dissolved under this subdivision may be extended by filing the initial corporate registration with the secretary of state and payment of a $25 fee. The extension relates back to December 31, 1997.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.821.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.139.0">1989 c 304 s 139</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.821.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.120.0">1989 c 304 s 120</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.205.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 205</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.39.0">1990 c 488 s 39</a>,40; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/205/#laws.0.14.0">1991 c 205 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/503/#laws.0.14.0">1992 c 503 s 14</a>,15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/137/#laws.0.10.0">1997 c 137 s 10</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.823">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.823 ANNUAL CORPORATE RENEWAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.823.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Annual renewal.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The secretary of state may send annually to each corporation, using the information provided by the corporation pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/5.002">5.002</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/5.34">5.34</a> or the articles of incorporation, a notice announcing the need to file the annual renewal and informing the corporation that the annual renewal may be filed online and that paper filings may also be made, and informing the corporation that failing to file the annual renewal will result in an administrative dissolution of the corporation.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) Each calendar year beginning in the calendar year following the calendar year in which a corporation incorporates, a corporation must file with the secretary of state by December 31 of each calendar year a registration containing the information required by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/5.34">5.34</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.823.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Penalty.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A corporation that has failed to file a renewal pursuant to subdivision 1 must be dissolved by the secretary of state as described in paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) If the corporation has not filed the delinquent renewal, the secretary of state must issue a certificate of involuntary dissolution, and the certificate must be filed in the Office of the Secretary of State. The secretary of state must also make available in an electronic format the names of the dissolved corporations. A corporation dissolved in this manner is not entitled to the benefits of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.781">317A.781</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.823.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/395/#laws.0.10.0">2000 c 395 s 10</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.823.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.121.0">1989 c 304 s 121</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/335/#laws.1.206.0">1989 c 335 art 1 s 206</a>,207; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/488/#laws.0.41.0">1990 c 488 s 41</a>,42; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/205/#laws.0.15.0">1991 c 205 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/503/#laws.0.16.0">1992 c 503 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/48/#laws.0.4.0">1993 c 48 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/86/#laws.0.2.0">1993 c 86 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/128/#laws.3.5.0">1995 c 128 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/395/#laws.0.10.0">2000 c 395 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/251/#laws.0.7.0">2004 c 251 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/148/#laws.2.55.0">2007 c 148 art 2 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/203/#laws.0.11.0">2008 c 203 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.79.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 79</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.825">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.825 ACCEPTANCE OF REGISTRATION BY SECRETARY OF STATE.</h1>
        <p>The secretary of state may accept a registration under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.821">317A.821</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.823">317A.823</a> if the information on the registration is in substantial compliance with these sections, even if the information on the registration is not identical to equivalent information in the records of the secretary of state.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.825.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.122.0">1989 c 304 s 122</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.827">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.827 CONTINUATION FOR CERTAIN PURPOSES; REINSTATEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.827.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.</h2>
          <p>MS 1998 [Repealed by amendment, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/395/#laws.0.11.0">2000 c 395 s 11</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.827.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Attorney general powers continued.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation dissolved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.823">317A.823</a> continues for three years after the dissolution date for the sole purpose of supervision, investigation, and other actions by the attorney general under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/8.31">8.31</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/501B.40">501B.40</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/501B.41">501B.41</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.827.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>MS 1998 [Renumbered subdivision 1]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.827.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reinstatement.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation dissolved under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.823">317A.823</a> may retroactively reinstate its corporate existence by filing a single annual registration. Filing the annual registration with the secretary of state:</p>
          <p>(1) returns the corporation to active status as of the date of the dissolution;</p>
          <p>(2) validates contracts or other acts within the authority of the articles, and the corporation is liable for those contracts or acts; and</p>
          <p>(3) restores to the corporation all assets and rights of the corporation and its members to the extent they were held by the corporation and its members before the dissolution occurred, except to the extent that assets or rights were affected by acts occurring after the dissolution or sold or otherwise distributed after that time.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.827.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Renumbered subd 2]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.827.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.123.0">1989 c 304 s 123</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/340/#laws.2.12.0">1989 c 340 art 2 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/205/#laws.0.16.0">1991 c 205 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/503/#laws.0.17.0">1992 c 503 s 17</a>,18; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/48/#laws.0.5.0">1993 c 48 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/137/#laws.0.11.0">1997 c 137 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/395/#laws.0.11.0">2000 c 395 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/10/#laws.2.74.0">1Sp2001 c 10 art 2 s 74</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ACTIONS AGAINST CORPORATIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.901">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.901 SERVICE OF PROCESS ON CORPORATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.901.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Who may be served.</span></h2>
          <p>A process, notice, or demand required or permitted by law to be served upon a corporation may be served upon the registered agent, if any, of the corporation named in the articles, upon an officer of the corporation, or upon the secretary of state as provided in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/5.25">5.25</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.901.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/128/#laws.1.20.0">1995 c 128 art 1 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.901.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/128/#laws.1.20.0">1995 c 128 art 1 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.901.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/128/#laws.1.20.0">1995 c 128 art 1 s 20</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.901.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.124.0">1989 c 304 s 124</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/128/#laws.1.11.0">1995 c 128 art 1 s 11</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.903">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.903 STATE INTERESTED; PROCEEDINGS.</h1>
        <p>If it appears at any stage of a proceeding in a court in this state that the state is, or is likely to be, interested in the proceeding, or that it is a matter of general public interest, the court shall order that a copy of the complaint or petition be served upon the attorney general in the manner prescribed for serving a summons in a civil action. The attorney general shall intervene in a proceeding when the attorney general determines that the public interest requires it, whether or not the attorney general has been served.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.903.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.125.0">1989 c 304 s 125</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SPECIAL PROVISIONS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.905">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.905 CHAMBERS OF COMMERCE, BOARDS OF TRADE, EXCHANGES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.905.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Special purposes.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may be formed under this chapter to:</p>
          <p>(1) acquire and disseminate useful business information;</p>
          <p>(2) promote equitable principles of trade; or</p>
          <p>(3) establish, maintain, and enforce uniformity in the commercial usages, business transactions, and trade relations in the municipality in which it is located.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.905.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Arbitration of differences.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation, formed for a purpose in subdivision 1, may, through its articles or bylaws, arbitrate and adjust differences between:</p>
          <p>(1) the corporation and its members;</p>
          <p>(2) the members; or</p>
          <p>(3) a member and a third person who has given written consent.</p>
          <p>The corporation may take testimony, make awards, and enforce an award by a fine or by a forfeiture of the membership of a person or of the person's other rights or privileges.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.905.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Public markets.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation that is a chamber of commerce, board of trade, or exchange, and that maintains or operates a regular place of business or trading room for members only, in which the members buy, sell, or exchange grain, livestock, or other farm products for themselves or for others, is a public market.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.905.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Association or corporation defined.</span></h2>
          <p>As used in subdivisions 5 and 6, the words "association or corporation" include a cooperative corporation or association authorized to do business in this state.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.905.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Membership in public market.</span></h2>
          <p>Membership in a public market is open to a person, association, or corporation:</p>
          <p>(1) having a method of business operation or plan of organization that does not conflict with a reasonable rule of the public market; and</p>
          <p>(2) desiring to deal or trade in the commodities usually dealt in on the public market.</p>
          <p>Application for membership must be made in the manner provided in the articles or bylaws of the public market.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.905.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Rules and bylaws of public markets.</span></h2>
          <p>A public market may make reasonable rules, regulations, and bylaws, including provisions for membership fees and uniform reasonable assessments. A rule, regulation, or bylaw of a public market is unreasonable when it modifies a provision in the articles, constitution, or bylaws of an association or corporation, governing the distribution of profits to the shareholders or members of the association or corporation. Members of a public market shall comply with reasonable rules, regulations, and bylaws established by the market.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.905.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Monopoly in restraint of trade.</span></h2>
          <p>A public market is a monopoly in restraint of trade when it:</p>
          <p>(1) wrongfully or unreasonably refuses to admit or delays the admission of an applicant for membership;</p>
          <p>(2) discriminates, or causes another to discriminate, among members; or</p>
          <p>(3) violates this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.905.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Prosecution by attorney general.</span></h2>
          <p>When a public market is a monopoly in restraint of trade under subdivision 7, trading or dealing in it is prohibited, and the attorney general shall bring an action to terminate the existence of the corporation under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/317A.751">317A.751</a>, or sue to enjoin further operation of the market or further violations of this chapter.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.905.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.126.0">1989 c 304 s 126</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.907">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.907 CORPORATIONS TO SECURE OR MAINTAIN HOMES FOR DEPENDENT CHILDREN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.907.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purposes.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation may be formed for the following purposes:</p>
          <p>(1) securing homes for orphaned, homeless, abandoned, neglected, or mistreated children; or</p>
          <p>(2) establishing and maintaining homes for those children.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.907.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Certificate of trustworthiness.</span></h2>
          <p>When it files its articles with the secretary of state, the corporation shall file an accompanying certificate of the commissioner of children, youth, and families declaring that the corporation has complied with applicable rules of the commissioner of children, youth, and families governing the operation of child caring agencies or child caring institutions and is reputable and trustworthy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.907.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Comply with rules; open books to public inspection.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation formed under subdivision 1 shall:</p>
          <p>(1) comply with rules established by the commissioner of children, youth, and families to govern its operation; and</p>
          <p>(2) maintain the financial records of the corporation open to public inspection.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.907.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Visitorial powers of court.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon its own motion, or upon application, a court of equity has visitorial powers over the corporation, its affairs and officers.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.907.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Legal guardian.</span></h2>
          <p>If the commissioner of children, youth, and families currently certifies that a corporation formed for the purpose set out in subdivision 1, clause (1), is a licensed child caring agency complying with the rules established by the commissioner of children, youth, and families to govern its operation, the corporation has the power to become the guardian of a child in the manner prescribed for securing the guardianship of children in need of protection or services under chapter 260.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.907.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Expense reimbursement.</span></h2>
          <p>An organization, association, or society licensed by the commissioner of children, youth, and families may receive payment for expenses related to adoption services in an amount that fairly reflects the agency's reasonable and necessary expenses of:</p>
          <p>(1) adoptive counseling, whether or not legal adoption is completed;</p>
          <p>(2) provision of services to children before adoptive placement;</p>
          <p>(3) the supervision of children in the home until legal adoption is completed; or</p>
          <p>(4) expenses of a birth parent authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.55">259.55</a> if paid to the agency to forward to the birth parent.</p>
          <p>Only that part of the expenses may be requested that the person seeking to adopt is financially able to meet. No person may be barred from receiving a child for adoption because of inability to pay part of the expenses referred to in this subdivision. In addition to other reports as may be required, a licensed agency shall file annually with the commissioner of children, youth, and families a full accounting of expense reimbursement received under this subdivision, together with the record of the services given for which the reimbursement was made. If the person returns the child to the corporation, the person may not receive compensation for the care, clothing, or medical expenses of the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.907.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Exemption of property from taxation.</span></h2>
          <p>A corporation formed for one or both of the purposes set out in subdivision 1 and personal and real property owned by it are exempt from taxation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.907.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.127.0">1989 c 304 s 127</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/631/#laws.0.29.0">1994 c 631 s 29</a>,31; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.8.70.0">2024 c 80 art 8 s 70</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.317A.909">
        <h1 class="shn">317A.909 CORPORATIONS FOR RELIGIOUS PURPOSES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.909.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Benefits for members.</span></h2>
          <p>When authorized by its members or otherwise, a corporation formed for a religious purpose may provide directly or through a church benefits board for:</p>
          <p>(1) support and payment of benefits to its ministers, teachers, employees, or functionaries and to the ministers, teachers, employees, or functionaries of a nonprofit organization affiliated with it or under its jurisdiction;</p>
          <p>(2) payment of benefits to the surviving spouses, children, dependents, or other beneficiaries of the persons named in clause (1);</p>
          <p>(3) collection of contributions and other payments; or</p>
          <p>(4) creation, maintenance, investment, management, and disbursement of necessary endowment, reserve, and other funds for these purposes, including a trust fund or corporation that funds a "church plan" as defined in section 414(e) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended through December 31, 1988.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.909.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Insurance laws not applicable.</span></h2>
          <p>The insurance laws of this state do not apply to the operations of a corporation under subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.909.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Property exempt from taxation.</span></h2>
          <p>Except for property leased or used for profit, personal and real property that a religious corporation necessarily uses for a religious purpose is exempt from taxation.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.909.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/10/#laws.2.5.0">2005 c 10 art 2 s 5</a>]</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.909.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Church benefits board.</span></h2>
          <p>A "church benefits board" is an organization described in section 414(e)(3)(A) of the Internal Revenue Code of 1986, as amended through December 31, 1988, whether a civil law corporation or otherwise, the principal purpose or function of which is the administration or funding of a plan or program for the provision of retirement benefits or welfare benefits for the employees of a church or a convention or association of churches, if the organization is controlled by or associated with a church or a convention or association of churches.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.317A.909.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Church benefits board as trustee.</span></h2>
          <p>A church benefits board may act as trustee under a lawful trust and may act as agent for the performance of a lawful act relating to the purposes of the trust.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.317A.909.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/304/#laws.0.128.0">1989 c 304 s 128</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/43/#laws.0.1.0">2009 c 43 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
    </div>
